<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Bose–Einstein condensation at positive temperature in the three-dimensional dilute hard-sphere gas. For each fixed exclusion distance and every sufficiently small fixed density, there is a strictly positive temperature, independent of the volume, at which the exact canonical Gibbs state has a positive condensate fraction in the thermodynamic limit. The condensate occupies the constant orbital.

- **claim-fixed-parameter-condensation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $a>0$, there is $\rho_*(a)>0$ such that for each fixed $0<\rho<\rho_*(a)$ there is a fixed temperature $T=T(a,\rho)>0$ for which $$\liminf_{\substack{L\to\infty\\N/L^3\to\rho}} \frac{\langle u_{0,L},\gamma^{(1)}_{N,L,T}u_{0,L}\rangle}{N}>0.$$ The exclusion distance, density, and temperature are held fixed in this limit.
  - Explanation: The exact canonical hard-sphere state has positive constant-orbital occupation in the thermodynamic limit at a positive temperature fixed before the volume grows.
- **claim-scaled-occupation** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-scaled)): There are constants $\alpha_0\ge2$, $D_*<\infty$, and $c_*>0$ such that $B_{\rm occ}\ge c_*$ whenever the parameters satisfy <a href="#eq:scaled-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scaled-parameters">[eq:scaled-parameters]</a>, $D\ge D_*$, and $K$ is sufficiently large. The lower threshold for $K$ can be chosen locally uniformly for $D$ in $[D_*,\infty)$, uniformly in $\alpha\in[\alpha_0/2,2\alpha_0]$ and $0<T\le D^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: A positive occupation bound holds uniformly over the scaled temperature and exclusion-parameter ranges, with a locally uniform volume threshold.
- **claim-cube-exclusion-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cube)): Let $Q\subset\mathbb R^3$ be a cube of side $l$, and let $F\in H^1(Q^n)$ vanish almost everywhere whenever some pair has distance at most $r$. No boundary condition is imposed on the outer faces. Then $$\label{eq:cube-energy} \sum_{i=1}^n\int_{Q^n}\|\nabla_iF\|^2 \ge c_{\rm cube}\frac r{l^3}n(n-1)\int_{Q^n}\|F\|^2, \qquad c_{\rm cube}=\frac1{64\sqrt3}.$$ Consequently, for a torus hard-core form function and every fixed label set $I$, $$\label{eq:cell-energy} q_I[F]\ge c_{\rm cube}r\int\sum_v n_v^I(n_v^I-1)\|F\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Hard exclusion produces a local kinetic penalty for multiple particles in a cube and, after cell subdivision, for retained label counts on the torus.
- **claim-close-neighbor-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-close)): For $0<r<s<1$, every hard-core form function satisfies $$\label{eq:close-energy} q_I[F]\ge c_{\rm near}rs^{-3} \int\#\{i\in I:d_i^I\le2s\}\|F\|^2, \qquad c_{\rm near}=\frac{27}{262144\sqrt3}.$$
  - Explanation: The kinetic energy controls the number of retained particles with a nearby retained neighbor.
- **claim-kinetic-entropy-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy)): Let $O$ be an open subset of $\Lambda_K$ with Dirichlet kinetic energy, or the whole torus with periodic kinetic energy. For every fixed $a>0$ and every symmetric trace-one $j$-particle state $\sigma$ of finite kinetic energy, $$\label{eq:entropy-bound} a\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^j-\Delta_{O,i}\Bigr)\sigma-TS(\sigma) \ge -Tj-C_aT\|O\|(V^{-1}+T^{3/2}).$$ In particular $S(\sigma)<\infty$. The statement holds for all $T>0$, including $j=0$ with the vacuum convention.
  - Explanation: A kinetic-energy contribution controls the entropy of symmetric finite-energy states, including admissible hard-core states after zero extension.
- **claim-normalized-particle-insertion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-insertion)): Let $w$ be a real normalized smooth one-particle orbital and $F$ an $n$-particle hard-core form function. Suppose that on the support of $F$ at most $k$ old positions lie within distance $2r$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}w$. If $C\lVert w\rVert_\infty^2kr^3\le1/4$, there is an admissible $(n+1)$-particle function $\widetilde F$ of the same norm, with the same old-coordinate position marginal, such that $$\label{eq:insertion-cost} q_{n+1}[\widetilde F]\le q_n[F]+C\bigl( \lVert\nabla w\rVert_2^2+\lVert w\rVert_\infty^2kr\bigr)\lVert F\rVert_2^2.$$ No symmetry in the new coordinate is asserted. Symmetry among the old coordinates is preserved whenever $F$ has it.
  - Explanation: A normalized insertion multiplier preserves the old position marginal and norm while bounding the additional kinetic energy. The inserted coordinate is not asserted to be symmetric with the old coordinates.
- **claim-particle-number-free-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-increments)): For sufficiently large $D$, uniformly in $K\ge100$, $0<T\le D^{-1}$, and the fixed $\alpha$ window, $$\begin{aligned} F_N&\ge c\alpha N,\label{eq:free-energy-lower}\\ F_{N+k}&\ge F_N+c\alpha k \qquad(0\le k\le N), \label{eq:free-energy-increase}\\ F_j^{(-W)}-F_{j-1}^{(-W)}&\le C\alpha \qquad(1\le j\le2N).\label{eq:free-energy-insertion} \end{aligned}$$ The last estimate holds for every bounded measurable $W\ge0$, with a constant independent of $W$. All form domains through particle number $2N$ are nonempty. The constants $c,C$ can be numerical; only the large-$D$ threshold depends on the fixed window for $\alpha$.
  - Explanation: The free energy has a positive extensive lower bound, positive lower increments above the reference particle number, and uniformly bounded upper insertion costs in bounded attractive fields.
- **claim-two-color-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-localization)): Let $p,q$ be real Lipschitz functions with $p^2+q^2=1$, and suppose $p$ is compactly supported in an open set $O$, or take $O=\Lambda_K$. For a finite-energy symmetric $J$-particle state $\sigma$, map each coordinate by $f\mapsto(pf,qf)$ and dephase by the number $j$ of particles in the first color. Denote its distribution by $w_j$ and the conditional first- and second-color marginal states by $\sigma_{i,j}$ and $\sigma_{o,j}$. For every bounded field $W\ge0$, $$\label{eq:two-color} \begin{split} \mathcal F_J^{(-W)}(\sigma) +\mathbb E_\sigma n_{\|\nabla p\|^2+\|\nabla q\|^2} \ge\sum_{j=0}^Jw_j\bigl[ \mathcal F_j^{(-W)}(\sigma_{i,j})+ \mathcal F_{J-j}^{(-W)}(\sigma_{o,j})\bigr]-TH(w). \end{split}$$ The inside states also satisfy the Dirichlet condition in $O$. Terms with $w_j=0$ are omitted, and vacuum states are allowed. Moreover $$\label{eq:color-entropy} \sum_jjw_j=\mathbb E_\sigma n_{p^2},\qquad H(w)\le-\log(1-e^{-1})+\sum_jjw_j.$$
  - Explanation: Spatial color localization splits the free energy into admissible species marginals with an explicit gradient cost and a color-entropy correction.
- **claim-diagonal-gibbs-tilt** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tilt)): For a bounded real symmetric Lipschitz function $G$ of $N$ positions, set $$g=e^G,\qquad M_G=\mathbb E_\mu e^{2G},\qquad \sigma_g=M_G^{-1}g\Gamma g.$$ Then $$\label{eq:diagonal-tilt} \mathcal F_N(\sigma_g)-F_N \le\mathbb E_{\sigma_g}\|\nabla G\|^2 +T\bigl(2\mathbb E_{\sigma_g}G-\log M_G\bigr).$$ Here $\|\nabla G\|^2=\sum_i\|\nabla_iG\|^2$.
  - Explanation: A bounded Lipschitz diagonal tilt incurs a free-energy excess controlled by its gradient and the associated classical change of density.
- **claim-weighted-local-penalty** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-local-penalty)): There are $c,C>0$, independent of $b$, such that for every $0\le J\le N$, every bounded measurable $0\le W\le1$, and every finite-energy symmetric trace-one state $\sigma$, $$\label{eq:local-penalty} \mathcal F_J^{(-W)}(\sigma)-F_J^{(-W)} \ge\alpha\left[\mathbb E_\sigma(f_A+cb^{-3}k_A)-CD\|A\|\right].$$ The threshold on $D$ may depend on $b$, but the displayed constants do not.
  - Explanation: The local free-energy excess controls both an exponentially weighted position count and an enlarged close-neighbor count near prescribed sites.
- **claim-equilibrium-field-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-field-density)): In the $J$-particle equilibrium state with $0\le J\le N$ and $0\le W\le1$, every set $A$ of sites satisfies $$\label{eq:field-density} \mathbb En_{\mathbf 1_{\bigcup_{v\in A}B_v}}\le CD\|A\|.$$
  - Explanation: A bounded attractive field preserves a uniform upper bound on the expected occupation of any prescribed union of cells.
- **claim-joint-cell-shortages** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-low-count)): One can choose $\alpha_0$ sufficiently large and then $\ell>0$ sufficiently small so that, for every set of sites $A$ and all sufficiently large $D$, $$\label{eq:low-count} \mu\{n_v<\ell D\text{ for every }v\in A\} \le e^{-cD\|A\|}.$$ The constants and thresholds are uniform in $K\ge100$ and $0<T\le D^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous particle shortages on prescribed cells have an exponentially small probability under the equilibrium position law, without conditioning on outside positions.
- **claim-joint-close-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-close-count)): For every fixed $\theta>0$, one can choose $b>0$ sufficiently small and then $D$ sufficiently large so that, for every set of sites $A$, $$\label{eq:close-count-tail} \mu\{C_v\ge\theta D\text{ for every }v\in A\} \le p_D^{\|A\|},\qquad p_D=\exp(-c\theta bD^{2/3})\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $c>0$ can be numerical, and the bounds remain uniform in the temperature and volume ranges above.
  - Explanation: After choosing the separation parameter small and the scaled density large, prescribed simultaneous close-count failures have an exponential bound whose base tends to zero.
- **claim-thermal-purification** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-purification)): Equation <a href="#eq:thermal-slab-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:thermal-slab-law">[eq:thermal-slab-law]</a> defines a probability measure. At every fixed time $s\in[0,\delta]$, its position marginal is $$\mu(\,\mathrm dX)=Z_N^{-1}B_\beta^{\rm sym}(X,X)\,\,\mathrm dX.$$ The joint law of $X(t)$ and $W'$ has density $\Phi(X,W')^2$, where $$\label{eq:thermal-Phi} \Phi(X,W')=Z_N^{-1/2}B_{\beta/2}^{\rm sym}(X,W').$$ This function is nonnegative, has squared integral one, and is symmetric separately in $X$ and $W'$. The constant-orbital occupation fraction of the scaled Gibbs state is $$\label{eq:occupation-purification} B_{\rm occ} =\frac1V\int\left\|\int_{\Lambda_K} \Phi(x,X',W')\,\,\mathrm dx\right\|^2\,\mathrm dX'\,\,\mathrm dW',$$ where $X'\in\Lambda_K^{N-1}$ is an ordered configuration.
  - Explanation: The short thermal path law is normalized, has equilibrium position marginals, and realizes the square-root Gibbs kernel as a positive purification retaining the bath and the additional configuration.
- **claim-free-path-regularity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-free-cutoffs)): Fix $t>0$. For every $q>0$, a free Brownian path on $[0,2t]$ fails regularity with probability at most $C_{q,t}D^{-q}$, uniformly in its starting point, $K$, and the allowed $\alpha$-window. The same assertion holds for a Euclidean bridge of duration $2t$ with bounded endpoint displacement, and for two independent Euclidean bridge legs of duration $t$ joined at a prescribed midpoint, when their three prescribed points lie in a fixed bounded region. Constants and the lower threshold on $D$ may depend on that region and $t$, but $C_{\rm reg}$ is a single numerical constant for all these laws. If the three points lie in the radius-$3$ ball about a site $v$, the probability that the two-leg path leaves the radius-$10$ ball about $v$ is at most $Ce^{-c/t}$ for $0<t\le1$.
  - Explanation: Free Brownian motion and the specified Euclidean bridges satisfy the regularity tests except with arbitrarily high inverse-power probability bounds. Confined prescribed endpoints also give a small escape probability.
- **claim-joint-certificate-supply** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-certificates)): For each prescribed $p_1>0$, one can choose $H_1$ large, then $t>0$ sufficiently small, and then $D_0$ sufficiently large, such that, for every set $A$ of distinct cell centers, $$\label{eq:joint-certificates} \mathcal R\{\text{no site of }A\text{ has a certificate}\} \le p_1^{\|A\|}$$ whenever $D\ge D_0$, $0<T\le D^{-1}$, and $K$ is sufficiently large. The choices are uniform over the $\alpha$-window. After $H_1$ is fixed, $t$ may be decreased further before increasing $D_0$.
  - Explanation: The full thermal sample supplies certificates with an arbitrarily small joint failure base after choosing the visitor threshold, path duration, and density threshold in the stated order.
- **claim-localized-marked-kernel** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-kernel)): For a fixed numerical $c_1>1$, which can be taken to be $3$, $$\label{eq:marked-kernel} \mathcal K_n(y,z)\le l_n p_n(y)p_n(z) [e^{c_1\delta\Delta_{O_n}}](y,z), \qquad l_n=C\lambda(n+1)e^{C\lambda(n+1)} e^{-cn^2/t}\varepsilon^{1/3},$$ where $\Delta_{O_n}$ has Dirichlet boundary conditions, or periodic conditions if $O_n=\Lambda_K$. The three choices of $E$ admit respectively $$\label{eq:path-epsilon} \varepsilon=1,\qquad \varepsilon=Ce^{-c/t},\qquad \varepsilon=C_tD^{-12}.$$ The constants for the first two choices are uniform for $0<t\le1$; the irregularity constant may depend on the fixed $t$ and the previously chosen spatial parameters.
  - Explanation: A marked free-path kernel is bounded by a spatially localized heat kernel with Gaussian bin suppression and a factor reflecting the rarity of the chosen trajectory event.
- **claim-lipschitz-sandwich-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sandwich)): For the preceding finite-color space and operators, set $$W_0=2a/\delta+c_1\|\nabla a\|^2,\qquad \widetilde{\mathbb H}=\mathbb H-\sum_{i=1}^N W_0(x_i).$$ For every real $q>0$, $$\label{eq:all-q-sandwich} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathcal H_e}X_0^q \le\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathcal H_e}e^{-q\delta\widetilde{\mathbb H}}.$$
  - Explanation: Every positive trace power of the Lipschitz sandwich is bounded by the heat trace of the enlarged Hamiltonian with the specified attractive field.
- **claim-interacting-trajectory-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-trajectory-moment)): Use any of the three events $E$ and the coefficients $l_n,\eta_n,M_n$ from Equations <a href="#eq:marked-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:marked-kernel">[eq:marked-kernel]</a> and <a href="#eq:color-gaps" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:color-gaps">[eq:color-gaps]</a>. For a sufficiently large fixed constant $C_0$, assume $$\label{eq:color-smallness} \frac{C_0}{\delta}\sum_{n\ge0}\eta_n\le1.$$ Then for every nonempty set $A$ of sites, $$\begin{aligned} &\log\mathbb E_{\mathcal R}\exp\left( \lambda\sum_{i=1}^N J_A(\omega_i)\mathbf 1_E(\omega_i)\right) \notag\\ &\quad\le CD\|A\|\sum_{n\ge0}(n+1)^3\eta_n +C\delta T\|A\|(V^{-1}+T^{3/2}) \sum_{n\ge0}(n+1)^3e^{-\beta M_n/2}. \label{eq:trajectory-moment} \end{aligned}$$ All constants are independent of $n_*,K,D,T$ and $0<t\le1$, with the stated $t$-dependence retained in $\varepsilon$ for irregularity.
  - Explanation: Under the color-smallness condition, the interacting marked-trajectory moment has a cost proportional to the prescribed site count rather than the full volume.
- **claim-separated-gaussian-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-packing)): Let $y_1,\ldots,y_m\in\mathbb R^3$ have pairwise distances at least $s_0$, where $m\ge\ell D/2$ and $s_0=bD^{-1/3}$. Fix $u\in\mathbb R^3$ and $0<s\le t/2$. The average of the time-$s$ densities of duration-$t$ Brownian bridges from $y_i$ to $u$ is bounded everywhere by $$\label{bridge:packed-density} C\left(1+\frac{1}{D s^{3/2}}\right).$$ Here $C$ depends on $b,\ell$, but not on $t,s,D$ or $u$.
  - Explanation: Separated initial endpoints bound the averaged intermediate density of bridges with a common prescribed terminal point.
- **claim-averaged-obstacle-collision** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-averaged-hit)): Fix $m\ge\ell D/2$ pairs $(y_i,z_i)$ with $y_i\in B_v$ and $\|z_i-v\|\le3$. Assume that each of the two endpoint lists is $s_0$-separated. Let $c$ be a deterministic regular torus path whose outer endpoints are at distance greater than $s_0$ from the corresponding endpoints of every label under consideration. Then, for every target cell $B$, $$\label{bridge:averaged-estimate} \frac1m\sum_{i=1}^m Q_{y_i,z_i}^{B}(\mathcal C_v\cap\mathcal H(c)) \le Ctr+C\frac{rD^{3\gamma}}{D\sqrt{h_0}}.$$ The same conclusion holds if $c$ is the original path of one of these labels: omit that label’s summand and retain the divisor $m$, assuming endpoint separation for all the remaining labels. The constant $C$ may depend on $b,\ell,C_{\rm reg}$, but is independent of small $t$.
  - Explanation: Averaging over separated endpoint pairs and target-cell midpoints controls collision with a deterministic regular obstacle. A self-label may be omitted while retaining the original divisor.
- **claim-single-replacement-collision** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-single-hit)): Let $y\in B_v$, $\|z-v\|\le3$, and let $c$ be a deterministic regular path satisfying $d(y,c(0))>s_0$ and $d(z,c(2t))>s_0$. For either $Q=Q_{y,z}^{B}$, with any target cell $B$, or $Q=Q_{y,z}^{0}$, one has $$\label{bridge:single-estimate} Q(\mathcal C_v\cap\mathcal H(c)) \le Ctr+C\frac{rD^{3\gamma}}{\sqrt{h_0}} \le C\alpha\bigl(tD^{-1}+D^{-1/4}\bigr).$$ Thus this probability tends to zero with $D$ for each fixed $t$.
  - Explanation: The collision probability of a confined regular replacement against a regular endpoint-separated obstacle tends to zero at fixed path duration, for both bridge laws stated.
- **claim-successful-label-supply** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-success-supply)): Consider a collection of deterministic torus paths with endpoints $(y_j,z_j)$, and fix a cell $v$. Suppose that at most $H_1D$ of these paths visit the ball of radius $12$ about $v$, and that every visiting path is regular. Suppose a list of $m\ge\ell D/2$ labels satisfies $y_i\in B_v$, $\|z_i-v\|\le3$, and that both endpoints of every listed label are at distance greater than $s_0$ from every other endpoint in the corresponding full configuration.* *Call a trial for label $i$ successful if it satisfies $\mathcal C_v$ and avoids every obstacle path except the original path of $i$. For sufficiently small fixed $t>0$ and then sufficiently large $D$, at least $9m/10$ listed labels have the following property: for each of the seven target cells $B$, the set $$\label{bridge:successful-target-set} T_{i,B}=\{u\in B: Q_{y_i,z_i}^u(\text{successful trial})\ge9/10\}$$ has volume at least $99/100$. All choices are uniform in the deterministic collection, $v$, and $\alpha\in[\alpha_0/2,2\alpha_0]$.
  - Explanation: Most listed labels admit large successful-midpoint sets in every target cell after the duration is chosen small and the density large.
- **claim-certified-useful-labels** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-useful-labels)): At a cell with a certificate there are at least $\ell D/2$ eligible labels. After $t$ has been chosen sufficiently small and $D$ sufficiently large, at least nine tenths of these labels satisfy the following stronger property: for every target cell $B_w$, $$\label{group:full-success} \left\|\left\{u\in B_w: Q_i^u(C_i(F_i^{\rm all}))\ge0.9\right\}\right\|\ge0.99.$$ Here $F_i^{\rm all}$ consists of all other trajectories in the sampled configuration, and $\|\cdot\|$ denotes spatial volume.
  - Explanation: A full-sample certificate supplies many eligible labels, and most of the entire eligible list has large successful target sets against all other sampled trajectories.
- **claim-exact-exposed-conditional-law** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-law)): For almost every exposed datum the normalizers in Equation <a href="#group:reference" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="group:reference">[group:reference]</a> are positive, and the conditional law of the unexposed trajectories is $$\label{group:product-law} \lambda_{\mathcal I}(\,\mathrm d\omega_{\mathcal I}) =\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal I}} \mathbf 1_{\{\text{all pairs in $\mathcal I$ avoid each other}\}} \prod_{i\in\mathcal I}P_i(\,\mathrm d\omega_i), \qquad Z_{\mathcal I}>0.$$ All reference trajectories obey their own regularity and confinement cutoffs. Each label has at most $C M$ other labels in this group with which a collision can be possible, where $C$ is a fixed geometric constant.
  - Explanation: Almost every exposed environment gives positive normalizers and an exact conditioned product law for the remaining paths. Confinement bounds the possible collision partners per label.
- **claim-unconditional-good-cell-supply** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-group-supply)): For any prescribed $p>0$, the constants before $M$ can be chosen as above, and then $M$ can be chosen sufficiently large, so that for sufficiently large $D$ and $K$, $$\label{group:bad-bound} \mathbb P_{\mathcal B}\{A\subset\{\text{bad sites}\}\}\le p^{\|A\|}$$ for every set $A$ of distinct sites. The bound is uniform in the group index, $0<T\le D^{-1}$, and the allowed $\alpha$-window. It remains valid if $M$ is increased before increasing the lower threshold on $D$.
  - Explanation: The unconditional exposed-environment law has an arbitrarily small joint bad-site base after the group size is chosen large and the density and volume thresholds are increased.
- **claim-one-path-density-compatibility** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-group-comparison)): There is a constant $G\ge2$, fixed after $t$ and independent of $D,K,M$, such that $$\label{group:density-bound} \Pi_i^U\ll P_i,\qquad 0\le\frac{\,\mathrm d\Pi_i^U}{\,\mathrm dP_i}\le G, \qquad \int\frac{\,\mathrm d\Pi_i^U}{\,\mathrm dP_i}\,\mathrm dP_i=1.$$ There is also $e_D\to0$, uniform in the exposed datum, with the following property. Fix any regular, confined trajectory of a distinct label in $\mathcal I$, keeping its recorded endpoints. Under either $P_i$ or $\Pi_i^U$, the probability of colliding with that deterministic trajectory is at most $e_D$. Its label need not be accessible. One may take $$\label{group:eta} e_D=C_t\bigl(tr+rD^{3\gamma}h_0^{-1/2}\bigr) \le C_t' D^{-1/4}.$$
  - Explanation: Accessible proposals have uniformly bounded densities relative to their reference laws and small collision probability against every permitted deterministic opponent with a distinct label.
- **claim-good-routes-annealed-encounters** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-good-paths)): Fix an integer $R\ge1$. There are constants $p_0,\kappa>0$, $C_{\rm path}<\infty$, and an integer $K_0$, depending only on $R$, with the following property. Suppose $K\ge K_0$ and <a href="#lattice:bad-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lattice:bad-law">[lattice:bad-law]</a> holds with $p\le p_0$. For every admissible ordered pair $(v,w)$ there is a $\mathcal D$-measurable event $\mathcal E_{v,w}$ of probability at least $1/2$. On this event there is a measurable probability kernel on simple good nearest-neighbor paths from $v$ to $w$. Two independent draws from this kernel, conditional on $\mathcal D$, satisfy $$\label{eq:lattice-encounter} \mathbb E\left[ \mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_{v,w}} \mathbb E\left[e^{\kappa J_R(\pi,\pi')}\mid\mathcal D\right] \right]\le C_{\rm path}.$$ The constants are uniform in the datum, its law, and the endpoint pair. For every sufficiently large $K$, at least $\upsilon K^6$ ordered pairs are admissible.
  - Explanation: A sparse dependent bad set admits measurable random simple good routes for admissible endpoint pairs, with an exponential encounter bound averaged over one common environment and independent route draws.
- **claim-folded-skeleton-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-skeleton)): For two independent skeletons just constructed, there are absolute constants $c_{\rm hit}>0$ and $C_{\rm hit}<\infty$ such that, for every integer $R_s\ge1$, $$\label{lattice:imported-hit} \mathbb Ee^{\alpha_sJ_{\rm occ}^{(R_s)}}\le C_{\rm hit}, \qquad \alpha_s=\frac{c_{\rm hit}}{(1+R_s)^2}.$$ For every fixed $B>0$, there are $\xi(B)>0$ and $C(B)<\infty$ such that $$\label{lattice:imported-soft} \mathbb Ee^{\xi(B)S_B(\Gamma,\Gamma')}\le C(B).$$ Both estimates are uniform in $K$ and the admissible endpoint pair.
  - Explanation: Independent folded skeletons have uniform exponential moments for encounter occurrences and for a soft-distance statistic.
- **claim-exterior-good-shell** (classification, [source](#stmt-lattice-boundary)): The set $\operatorname{fill}(C)$ lies in the coordinate bounding box of $C$. The exterior vertices at maximum distance one from $C$ form a nearest-neighbor connected set, denoted $\partial_*^{\rm ext}C$, with at most $26\|C\|$ vertices. If $C$ is a $*$-component of a larger bad set, every vertex of this shell is good.
  - Explanation: The filled finite component stays within its coordinate box, and its exterior shell is connected, has controlled size, and is good when the component is maximal in the bad set.
- **claim-small-periodic-component-lifts** (classification, [source](#stmt-lattice-small-lifts)): Suppose $K>2a$, and a range-$a$ component on $\mathcal L_K$ has $m$ vertices with $am<K$. Each of its lifts to $\mathbb Z^3$ is a finite component projecting bijectively onto it. The coordinate span of a lift is at most $a(m-1)$.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently small periodic component has consistent finite lifts projecting bijectively, with coordinate span controlled by its size.
- **claim-common-measure-affinity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-affinity)): Let $P,Q$ be finite nonnegative measures and let $\nu$ be a finite measure of mass $m>0$, absolutely continuous with respect to both. If $$\int\left(\frac{\,\mathrm d\nu}{\,\mathrm dP}\right)^2\,\mathrm dP\le C_P, \qquad \int\left(\frac{\,\mathrm d\nu}{\,\mathrm dQ}\right)^2\,\mathrm dQ\le C_Q,$$ then $\mathop{\mathrm{Aff}}(P,Q)\ge m^2/\sqrt{C_PC_Q}$.
  - Explanation: A positive common measure with bounded squared likelihood integrals forces a positive affinity between the two reference measures.
- **claim-disjoint-tag-normalization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tag-normalization)): For each ordered pair of cells, $$\label{cmp:group-affinity} \mathop{\mathrm{Aff}}(P_{vw},Q_{vw}) \ge\frac1D\sum_{g=1}^{G_D}\mathop{\mathrm{Aff}}(\widehat P_g,\widehat Q_g).$$
  - Explanation: Disjoint groups of summed tag contributions give a lower bound on cell affinity after projection, with the density factor supplied by the full label normalization.
- **claim-conditional-normalizer-cancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cancellation)): Consider two changes $S,T$ of the preceding kind, with $G\ge1$, and put $U=S\cup T$. Suppose that the following conditions hold.* 1. *There is $\chi\in[0,1)$ with two insertion bounds. In the reference system, inserting any coordinate $i\in U$ from $P_i$ satisfies all reference checks incident to $i$ with probability at least $1-\chi$. In the proposal system of either change, inserting a whole factor from $\Pi_{S,i}$ or $\Pi_{T,i}$ satisfies all checks of that proposal incident to its carried coordinates with probability at least $1-\chi$. Each bound is uniform in every fixed choice of the other individually permitted coordinates of the respective system, and remains valid after deleting checks or coordinates as specified above.* 2. *There are decompositions $S=C_S\sqcup F_S$ and $T=C_T\sqcup F_T$, with $F_S\cap T=F_T\cap S=\varnothing$. No coordinate carried by an $F_S$ factor has a check with any coordinate carried by a $T$ factor, or with the original primary coordinates indexed by $T$; the corresponding assertion holds with $S,T$ interchanged. The reference coordinates in $F_S,F_T$ also have no mutual checks.* *Then $$\label{cmp:cancellation-bound} \int L_SL_T\,\mathrm d\lambda \le G^{\|S\cap T\|}(1-\chi)^{-2(\|C_S\|+\|C_T\|)}.$$ It is enough that the assumptions hold for almost every fixed admissible configuration outside $U$.
  - Explanation: Uniform insertion compatibility and separation of far factors cancel the far conditional normalizers. The remaining likelihood cost depends on overlap and near factors.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cube",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q\\subset\\mathbb R^3$ be a cube of side $l$, and let $F\\in H^1(Q^n)$ vanish almost everywhere whenever some pair has distance at most $r$. No boundary condition is imposed on the outer faces. Then $$\\label{eq:cube-energy}\n \\sum_{i=1}^n\\int_{Q^n}|\\nabla_iF|^2\n \\ge c_{\\rm cube}\\frac r{l^3}n(n-1)\\int_{Q^n}|F|^2,\n \\qquad c_{\\rm cube}=\\frac1{64\\sqrt3}.$$ Consequently, for a torus hard-core form function and every fixed label set $I$, $$\\label{eq:cell-energy}\n q_I[F]\\ge c_{\\rm cube}r\\int\\sum_v n_v^I(n_v^I-1)|F|^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $w$ be a real normalized smooth one-particle orbital and $F$ an $n$-particle hard-core form function. Suppose that on the support of $F$ at most $k$ old positions lie within distance $2r$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}w$. If $C\\lVert w\\rVert_\\infty^2kr^3\\le1/4$, there is an admissible $(n+1)$-particle function $\\widetilde F$ of the same norm, with the same old-coordinate position marginal, such that $$\\label{eq:insertion-cost}\n q_{n+1}[\\widetilde F]\\le q_n[F]+C\\bigl(\n       \\lVert\\nabla w\\rVert_2^2+\\lVert w\\rVert_\\infty^2kr\\bigr)\\lVert F\\rVert_2^2.$$ No symmetry in the new coordinate is asserted.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-localization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p,q$ be real Lipschitz functions with $p^2+q^2=1$, and suppose $p$ is compactly supported in an open set $O$, or take $O=\\Lambda_K$. For a finite-energy symmetric $J$-particle state $\\sigma$, map each coordinate by $f\\mapsto(pf,qf)$ and dephase by the number $j$ of particles in the first color. Denote its distribution by $w_j$ and the conditional first- and second-color marginal states by $\\sigma_{i,j}$ and $\\sigma_{o,j}$. For every bounded field $W\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:two-color}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathcal F_J^{(-W)}(\\sigma)\n  +\\mathbb E_\\sigma n_{|\\nabla p|^2+|\\nabla q|^2}\n \\ge\\sum_{j=0}^Jw_j\\bigl[\n  \\mathcal F_j^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{i,j})+\n  \\mathcal F_{J-j}^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{o,j})\\bigr]-TH(w).\n \\end{split}$$ The inside states also satisfy the Dirichlet condition in $O$. Terms with $w_j=0$ are omitted, and vacuum states are allowed. Moreover $$\\label{eq:color-entropy}\n \\sum_jjw_j=\\mathbb E_\\sigma n_{p^2},\\qquad\n H(w)\\le-\\log(1-e^{-1})+\\sum_jjw_j.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-localization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p,q$ be real Lipschitz functions with $p^2+q^2=1$, and suppose $p$ is compactly supported in an open set $O$, or take $O=\\Lambda_K$. For a finite-energy symmetric $J$-particle state $\\sigma$, map each coordinate by $f\\mapsto(pf,qf)$ and dephase by the number $j$ of particles in the first color. Denote its distribution by $w_j$ and the conditional first- and second-color marginal states by $\\sigma_{i,j}$ and $\\sigma_{o,j}$. For every bounded field $W\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:two-color}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathcal F_J^{(-W)}(\\sigma)\n  +\\mathbb E_\\sigma n_{|\\nabla p|^2+|\\nabla q|^2}\n \\ge\\sum_{j=0}^Jw_j\\bigl[\n  \\mathcal F_j^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{i,j})+\n  \\mathcal F_{J-j}^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{o,j})\\bigr]-TH(w).\n \\end{split}$$ The inside states also satisfy the Dirichlet condition in $O$. Terms with $w_j=0$ are omitted, and vacuum states are allowed. Moreover $$\\label{eq:color-entropy}\n \\sum_jjw_j=\\mathbb E_\\sigma n_{p^2},\\qquad\n H(w)\\le-\\log(1-e^{-1})+\\sum_jjw_j.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $p,q$ be real Lipschitz functions with $p^2+q^2=1$, and suppose $p$ is compactly supported in an open set $O$, or take $O=\\Lambda_K$. For a finite-energy symmetric $J$-particle state $\\sigma$, map each coordinate by $f\\mapsto(pf,qf)$ and dephase by the number $j$ of particles in the first color. Denote its distribution by $w_j$ and the conditional first- and second-color marginal states by $\\sigma_{i,j}$ and $\\sigma_{o,j}$. For every bounded field $W\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:two-color}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathcal F_J^{(-W)}(\\sigma)\n  +\\mathbb E_\\sigma n_{|\\nabla p|^2+|\\nabla q|^2}\n \\ge\\sum_{j=0}^Jw_j\\bigl[\n  \\mathcal F_j^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{i,j})+\n  \\mathcal F_{J-j}^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{o,j})\\bigr]-TH(w).\n \\end{split}$$ The inside states also satisfy the Dirichlet condition in $O$. Terms with $w_j=0$ are omitted, and vacuum states are allowed. Moreover $$\\label{eq:color-entropy}\n \\sum_jjw_j=\\mathbb E_\\sigma n_{p^2},\\qquad\n H(w)\\le-\\log(1-e^{-1})+\\sum_jjw_j.$$",
      "explanation": "Spatial color localization splits the free energy into admissible species marginals with an explicit gradient cost and a color-entropy correction.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-close-count",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $\\theta>0$, one can choose $b>0$ sufficiently small and then $D$ sufficiently large so that, for every set of sites $A$, $$\\label{eq:close-count-tail}\n \\mu\\{C_v\\ge\\theta D\\text{ for every }v\\in A\\}\n \\le p_D^{|A|},\\qquad\n p_D=\\exp(-c\\theta bD^{2/3})\\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $c>0$ can be numerical, and the bounds remain uniform in the temperature and volume ranges above.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-purification",
        "exact_quote": "Equation <a href=\"#eq:thermal-slab-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:thermal-slab-law\">[eq:thermal-slab-law]</a> defines a probability measure. At every fixed time $s\\in[0,\\delta]$, its position marginal is $$\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dX)=Z_N^{-1}B_\\beta^{\\rm sym}(X,X)\\,\\,\\mathrm dX.$$ The joint law of $X(t)$ and $W'$ has density $\\Phi(X,W')^2$, where $$\\label{eq:thermal-Phi}\n \\Phi(X,W')=Z_N^{-1/2}B_{\\beta/2}^{\\rm sym}(X,W').$$ This function is nonnegative, has squared integral one, and is symmetric separately in $X$ and $W'$. The constant-orbital occupation fraction of the scaled Gibbs state is $$\\label{eq:occupation-purification}\n B_{\\rm occ}\n =\\frac1V\\int\\left|\\int_{\\Lambda_K}\n       \\Phi(x,X',W')\\,\\,\\mathrm dx\\right|^2\\,\\mathrm dX'\\,\\,\\mathrm dW',$$ where $X'\\in\\Lambda_K^{N-1}$ is an ordered configuration.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-purification",
          "exact_quote": "Equation <a href=\"#eq:thermal-slab-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:thermal-slab-law\">[eq:thermal-slab-law]</a> defines a probability measure. At every fixed time $s\\in[0,\\delta]$, its position marginal is $$\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dX)=Z_N^{-1}B_\\beta^{\\rm sym}(X,X)\\,\\,\\mathrm dX.$$ The joint law of $X(t)$ and $W'$ has density $\\Phi(X,W')^2$, where $$\\label{eq:thermal-Phi}\n \\Phi(X,W')=Z_N^{-1/2}B_{\\beta/2}^{\\rm sym}(X,W').$$ This function is nonnegative, has squared integral one, and is symmetric separately in $X$ and $W'$. The constant-orbital occupation fraction of the scaled Gibbs state is $$\\label{eq:occupation-purification}\n B_{\\rm occ}\n =\\frac1V\\int\\left|\\int_{\\Lambda_K}\n       \\Phi(x,X',W')\\,\\,\\mathrm dx\\right|^2\\,\\mathrm dX'\\,\\,\\mathrm dW',$$ where $X'\\in\\Lambda_K^{N-1}$ is an ordered configuration.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Equation <a href=\"#eq:thermal-slab-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:thermal-slab-law\">[eq:thermal-slab-law]</a> defines a probability measure. At every fixed time $s\\in[0,\\delta]$, its position marginal is $$\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dX)=Z_N^{-1}B_\\beta^{\\rm sym}(X,X)\\,\\,\\mathrm dX.$$ The joint law of $X(t)$ and $W'$ has density $\\Phi(X,W')^2$, where $$\\label{eq:thermal-Phi}\n \\Phi(X,W')=Z_N^{-1/2}B_{\\beta/2}^{\\rm sym}(X,W').$$ This function is nonnegative, has squared integral one, and is symmetric separately in $X$ and $W'$. The constant-orbital occupation fraction of the scaled Gibbs state is $$\\label{eq:occupation-purification}\n B_{\\rm occ}\n =\\frac1V\\int\\left|\\int_{\\Lambda_K}\n       \\Phi(x,X',W')\\,\\,\\mathrm dx\\right|^2\\,\\mathrm dX'\\,\\,\\mathrm dW',$$ where $X'\\in\\Lambda_K^{N-1}$ is an ordered configuration.",
      "explanation": "The short thermal path law is normalized, has equilibrium position marginals, and realizes the square-root Gibbs kernel as a positive purification retaining the bath and the additional configuration.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-cutoffs",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $t>0$. For every $q>0$, a free Brownian path on $[0,2t]$ fails regularity with probability at most $C_{q,t}D^{-q}$, uniformly in its starting point, $K$, and the allowed $\\alpha$-window. The same assertion holds for a Euclidean bridge of duration $2t$ with bounded endpoint displacement, and for two independent Euclidean bridge legs of duration $t$ joined at a prescribed midpoint, when their three prescribed points lie in a fixed bounded region. Constants and the lower threshold on $D$ may depend on that region and $t$, but $C_{\\rm reg}$ is a single numerical constant for all these laws. If the three points lie in the radius-$3$ ball about a site $v$, the probability that the two-leg path leaves the radius-$10$ ball about $v$ is at most $Ce^{-c/t}$ for $0<t\\le1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-cutoffs",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $t>0$. The choices are uniform over the $\\alpha$-window. After $H_1$ is fixed, $t$ may be decreased further before increasing $D_0$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-marked-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "For a fixed numerical $c_1>1$, which can be taken to be $3$, $$\\label{eq:marked-kernel}\n \\mathcal K_n(y,z)\\le l_n p_n(y)p_n(z)\n       [e^{c_1\\delta\\Delta_{O_n}}](y,z),\n \\qquad\n l_n=C\\lambda(n+1)e^{C\\lambda(n+1)}\n       e^{-cn^2/t}\\varepsilon^{1/3},$$ where $\\Delta_{O_n}$ has Dirichlet boundary conditions, or periodic conditions if $O_n=\\Lambda_K$.         "exact_quote": "Use any of the three events $E$ and the coefficients $l_n,\\eta_n,M_n$ from Equations <a href=\"#eq:marked-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:marked-kernel\">[eq:marked-kernel]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:color-gaps\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:color-gaps\">[eq:color-gaps]</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $y_1,\\ldots,y_m\\in\\mathbb R^3$ have pairwise distances at least $s_0$, where $m\\ge\\ell D/2$ and $s_0=bD^{-1/3}$. Fix $u\\in\\mathbb R^3$ and $0<s\\le t/2$. The average of the time-$s$ densities of duration-$t$ Brownian bridges from $y_i$ to $u$ is bounded everywhere by $$\\label{bridge:packed-density}\n C\\left(1+\\frac{1}{D s^{3/2}}\\right).$$ Here $C$ depends on $b,\\ell$, but not on $t,s,D$ or $u$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-averaged-hit",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $m\\ge\\ell D/2$ pairs $(y_i,z_i)$ with $y_i\\in B_v$ and $|z_i-v|\\le3$. Assume that each of the two endpoint lists is $s_0$-separated. Let $c$ be a deterministic regular torus path whose outer endpoints are at distance greater than $s_0$ from the corresponding endpoints of every label under consideration. Then, for every target cell $B$, $$\\label{bridge:averaged-estimate}\n \\frac1m\\sum_{i=1}^m\n Q_{y_i,z_i}^{B}(\\mathcal C_v\\cap\\mathcal H(c))\n \\le Ctr+C\\frac{rD^{3\\gamma}}{D\\sqrt{h_0}}.$$ The same conclusion holds if $c$ is the original path of one of these labels: omit that label’s summand and retain the divisor $m$, assuming endpoint separation for all the remaining labels. The constant $C$ may depend on $b,\\ell,C_{\\rm reg}$, but is independent of small $t$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-success-supply",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a collection of deterministic torus paths with endpoints $(y_j,z_j)$, and fix a cell $v$. Suppose a list of $m\\ge\\ell D/2$ labels satisfies $y_i\\in B_v$, $|z_i-v|\\le3$, and that both endpoints of every listed label are at distance greater than $s_0$ from every other endpoint in the corresponding full configuration.*\n\n*Call a trial for label $i$ successful if it satisfies $\\mathcal C_v$ and avoids every obstacle path except the original path of $i$. For sufficiently small fixed $t>0$ and then sufficiently large $D$, at least $9m/10$ listed labels have the following property: for each of the seven target cells $B$, the set $$\\label{bridge:successful-target-set}\n T_{i,B}=\\{u\\in B:\n     Q_{y_i,z_i}^u(\\text{successful trial})\\ge9/10\\}$$ has volume at least $99/100$.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-conditional-law",
        "exact_quote": "For almost every exposed datum the normalizers in Equation <a href=\"#group:reference\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"group:reference\">[group:reference]</a> are positive, and the conditional law of the unexposed trajectories is $$\\label{group:product-law}\n \\lambda_{\\mathcal I}(\\,\\mathrm d\\omega_{\\mathcal I})\n =\\frac{1}{Z_{\\mathcal I}}\n   \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\text{all pairs in $\\mathcal I$ avoid each other}\\}}\n   \\prod_{i\\in\\mathcal I}P_i(\\,\\mathrm d\\omega_i),\n \\qquad Z_{\\mathcal I}>0.$$ All reference trajectories obey their own regularity and confinement cutoffs.         "exact_quote": "For any prescribed $p>0$, the constants before $M$ can be chosen as above, and then $M$ can be chosen sufficiently large, so that for sufficiently large $D$ and $K$, $$\\label{group:bad-bound}\n \\mathbb P_{\\mathcal B}\\{A\\subset\\{\\text{bad sites}\\}\\}\\le p^{|A|}$$ for every set $A$ of distinct sites.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-group-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $G\\ge2$, fixed after $t$ and independent of $D,K,M$, such that $$\\label{group:density-bound}\n \\Pi_i^U\\ll P_i,\\qquad\n 0\\le\\frac{\\,\\mathrm d\\Pi_i^U}{\\,\\mathrm dP_i}\\le G,\n \\qquad \\int\\frac{\\,\\mathrm d\\Pi_i^U}{\\,\\mathrm dP_i}\\,\\mathrm dP_i=1.$$ There is also $e_D\\to0$, uniform in the exposed datum, with the following property. Fix any regular, confined trajectory of a distinct label in $\\mathcal I$, keeping its recorded endpoints. Under either $P_i$ or $\\Pi_i^U$, the probability of colliding with that deterministic trajectory is at most $e_D$. Its label need not be accessible. One may take $$\\label{group:eta}\n e_D=C_t\\bigl(tr+rD^{3\\gamma}h_0^{-1/2}\\bigr)\n \\le C_t' D^{-1/4}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $R\\ge1$. There are constants $p_0,\\kappa>0$, $C_{\\rm path}<\\infty$, and an integer $K_0$, depending only on $R$, with the following property. For every admissible ordered pair $(v,w)$ there is a $\\mathcal D$-measurable event $\\mathcal E_{v,w}$ of probability at least $1/2$. On this event there is a measurable probability kernel on simple good nearest-neighbor paths from $v$ to $w$. Two independent draws from this kernel, conditional on $\\mathcal D$, satisfy $$\\label{eq:lattice-encounter}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\n   \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E_{v,w}}\n   \\mathbb E\\left[e^{\\kappa J_R(\\pi,\\pi')}\\mid\\mathcal D\\right]\n       \\right]\\le C_{\\rm path}.$$ The constants are uniform in the datum, its law, and the endpoint pair. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Allowed hard-sphere configurations** ([source](#pm-h-3)): On the flat torus $\Lambda_L=(\mathbb R/L\mathbb Z)^3$, let $d_L$ denote Euclidean torus distance and set $$\Omega_{N,L,a} =\{(x_1,\ldots,x_N)\in\Lambda_L^N: d_L(x_i,x_j)>a\text{ whenever }i\ne j\}.$$
- **Hard-sphere Hamiltonian** ([source](#pm-h-3)): In units $\hbar^2/(2m)=k_B=1$, the hard-sphere Hamiltonian $H_{N,L,a}$ is the self-adjoint operator associated with $$q[f]=\sum_{i=1}^N\int_{\Omega_{N,L,a}}\|\nabla_i f\|^2, \qquad f\in H^1_0(\Omega_{N,L,a})\text{ symmetric}.$$
- **Canonical state and reduced density matrix** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $T>0$, its canonical state and one-particle density matrix are $$\Gamma_{N,L,T}= \frac{e^{-H_{N,L,a}/T}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-H_{N,L,a}/T}},\qquad \gamma^{(1)}_{N,L,T}=N\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{2,\ldots,N}\Gamma_{N,L,T}.$$
- **Constant orbital** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The constant orbital is $u_{0,L}=L^{-3/2}$.
- **Scaled parameters** ([source](#eq:scaled-parameters)): Let $K\ge100$ be an integer and let $\Lambda_K=(\mathbb R/K\mathbb Z)^3$. Write $$\label{eq:scaled-parameters} V=K^3,\qquad D=N/V,\qquad r=\alpha/D,\qquad \alpha\in[\alpha_0/2,2\alpha_0],\qquad 0<T\le D^{-1},\qquad \beta=T^{-1}.$$
- **Field-perturbed Hamiltonian and free energy** ([source](#sec:setup)): For a bounded measurable function $W\ge0$, set $$H_j^{(-W)}=H_j-\sum_{i=1}^jW(x_i),\qquad Z_j^{(-W)}=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta H_j^{(-W)}},\qquad F_j^{(-W)}=-T\log Z_j^{(-W)}.$$
- **Occupation fraction** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The occupation fraction is $$B_{\rm occ}=N^{-1}\langle u_0,\gamma_N^{(1)}u_0\rangle.$$
- **Regular path** ([source](#stmt-def-regular)): Fix a sufficiently large numerical constant $C_{\rm reg}$. A path on $[0,2t]$ is *regular* if $$\label{eq:regularity-tests} \max_j A_j\le D^\gamma, \qquad \frac1{2m_t}\sum_{j=1}^{2m_t}(1+A_j)^3\le C_{\rm reg},$$ and its lifted diameter in each endpoint window $[0,h_0]$ and $[2t-h_0,2t]$ is at most $s_0/8$.
- **Target-cell bridge law** ([source](#sec:bridges)): For $u$ in a target cell $B$, let $Q_{y,z}^u$ be the law obtained by joining two independent Euclidean Brownian bridges, of generator $\Delta$, from $y$ to $u$ and from $u$ to $z$, each of duration $t$.
- **Single-path exposed reference** ([source](#group:reference)): For $i\in\mathcal I$ at site $v$, let $B_i$ be the unconditioned Euclidean bridge law of duration $2t$ between its near endpoint lifts. Define $$\label{group:reference} P_i(\,\mathrm d\omega_i)= \frac{\mathbf 1_{C_i(F)}\,B_i(\,\mathrm d\omega_i)}{a_i}, \qquad a_i=B_i(C_i(F)).$$
- **Accessible label** ([source](#group:targets)): A label is *accessible* if $\|T_i(w)\|\ge0.99$ for all seven target cells.
- **Good and bad sites** ([source](#group:targets)): A site is *good* if $\|\mathcal A_v\|\ge M/2$, and *bad* otherwise.
- **Route encounter count** ([source](#lattice:contact-definition)): For two simple paths $\pi,\pi'$ define $$\label{lattice:contact-definition} J_R(\pi,\pi')= \sum_{z\in\pi}\sum_{z'\in\pi'} \mathbf 1_{\{d_\infty(z,z')\le R\}}.$$
- **Filled finite component** ([source](#pm-h-39)): For a finite $*$-connected nonempty set $C\subset\mathbb Z^3$, let $\operatorname{fill}(C)$ consist of $C$ and all finite nearest-neighbor components of $\mathbb Z^3\setminus C$.
- **Affinity of finite measures** ([source](#cmp:affinity-superadditive)): For finite nonnegative measures $P,Q$ on the same measurable space, define their affinity by $$\mathop{\mathrm{Aff}}(P,Q)=\int\sqrt{pq}\,\mathrm d\xi, \qquad p=\frac{\,\mathrm dP}{\,\mathrm d\xi},\quad q=\frac{\,\mathrm dQ}{\,\mathrm d\xi}, \quad \xi=P+Q.$$
- **Individually permitted configuration** ([source](#pm-h-46)): A configuration is *individually permitted* when each coordinate lies in its allowed set; this imposes no pairwise checks.

            "exact_quote": "#### A local penalty and density control in a field For a nonempty set of sites $A\\subset\\mathcal L_K$, define, for a configuration of any particle number $J\\le N$, $$\\begin{aligned} s_0&=bD^{-1/3},\\qquad 0<b<1,\\label{eq:s0}\\\\ f_A(X)&=\\sum_{i=1}^J e^{-d(x_i,A)},\\label{eq:weighted-count}\\\\ k_A(X)&=\\#\\{i:d(x_i,A)\\le6,\\ d_i\\le2s_0\\}. \\label{eq:localized-close-count} \\end{aligned}$$ The parameter $b$ will be chosen small after the low-count threshold has been fixed. We always increase $D$ so that $r<s_0<1/2$. <div id=\"prop:local-penalty\" class=\"proposition\"> **Proposition 10** (Weighted local free-energy penalty). *There are $c,C>0$, independent of $b$, such that for every $0\\le J\\le N$, every bounded measurable $0\\le W\\le1$, and every finite-energy symmetric trace-one state $\\sigma$, $$\\label{eq:local-penalty} \\mathcal F_J^{(-W)}(\\sigma)-F_J^{(-W)} \\ge\\alpha\\left[\\mathbb E_\\sigma(f_A+cb^{-3}k_A)-CD|A|\\right].$$ The threshold on $D$ may depend on $b$, but the displayed constants do not.* </div> <details id=\"proof-stmt-prop-local-penalty\"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-local-penalty</summary> *Proof.* We combine the exponential localization of OpenAI (2026b, sec. 3, “Exponential spatial localization”) [[HS](#ref-HS)] with the thermal entropy decomposition of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:localization\">8</a>. For $h\\ge0$, choose sine–cosine masks $p_h,q_h$ such that $p_h=1$ at distance at most $h$ from $A$, $p_h=0$ from distance $h+1$ onward, and $$|\\nabla p_h|^2+|\\nabla q_h|^2 \\le C\\mathbf 1_{\\{h<d(x,A)<h+1\\}}.$$ Take the inside region $O_h$ to be the open $(h+2)$-neighborhood, or the torus when appropriate. Its volume is at most $C(h+4)^3|A|$, and $p_h$ is compactly supported there. Apply Lemma <a href=\"#lem:localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:localization\">8</a>. Outside, the upper increments of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:increments\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:increments\">7</a> give $F_{J-j}^{(-W)}\\ge F_J^{(-W)}-C\\alpha j$. Inside, divide the kinetic energy into three fixed positive portions. One portion pays for the entire von Neumann entropy by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:entropy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:entropy\">5</a>. Apply Lemmas <a href=\"#lem:cube\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:cube\">3</a> and <a href=\"#lem:close\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:close\">4</a> to the other two, with $s=s_0$. The field costs at most $j$, while the color entropy costs at most $T(C+j)$ by <a href=\"#eq:color-entropy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:color-entropy\">[eq:color-entropy]</a>. Thus all losses linear in the inside number are bounded by $C\\alpha j$, since $T\\le1$ and $\\alpha\\ge1$. The remaining entropy error is at most $$CT\\bigl[1+|O_h|(V^{-1}+T^{3/2})\\bigr] \\le C(h+4)^3|A|.$$ Average with the probability density $e^{-h}\\,\\,\\mathrm dh$. For every position $x$, $$\\int_0^\\infty p_h(x)^2e^{-h}\\,\\,\\mathrm dh\\le e\\,e^{-d(x,A)}, \\quad \\int_0^\\infty\\mathbf 1_{\\{h<d(x,A)<h+1\\}}e^{-h}\\,\\,\\mathrm dh \\le e\\,e^{-d(x,A)}.$$ Consequently the averaged removal and localization costs are at most $C\\alpha\\mathbb E_\\sigma f_A$, and the residual entropy cost is at most $C\\alpha|A|$. To retain the positive terms, set $b_v=e^{-d(v,A)}$. If $h\\ge d(v,A)+1$, every coordinate in $B_v$ belongs to the first color with probability one. The averaged cube contribution is therefore at least $cr\\mathbb E_\\sigma\\sum_v b_vn_v(n_v-1)$. A particle counted by $k_A$ has a partner within $2s_0<1$; both lie at distance less than $7$ from $A$. For $h\\ge8$ both labels belong to the first color, so its close-neighbor inequality counts this particle. This yields at least $crs_0^{-3}\\mathbb E_\\sigma k_A=c\\alpha b^{-3}\\mathbb E_\\sigma k_A$. The color interpretation used here follows from the conditional independent choices in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:localization\">8</a>. We have proved $$\\mathcal F_J^{(-W)}(\\sigma)-F_J^{(-W)} \\ge\\mathbb E_\\sigma\\left[cr\\sum_vb_vn_v(n_v-1) -C\\alpha f_A+c\\alpha b^{-3}k_A\\right]-C\\alpha|A|.$$ Within a unit cell, distances to $A$ differ from the center distance by less than one. Hence $$f_A\\le e\\sum_v b_vn_v,\\qquad \\sum_v b_v\\le\\sum_{w\\in A}\\sum_v e^{-d(v,w)}\\le C|A|.$$ The final lattice sum is uniformly bounded on periodic unit grids. For $n\\ge0$ and $D\\ge1$, completing a scalar square gives $$\\frac cD n(n-1)-(C+1)e n\\ge-C'D.$$ Multiply by $\\alpha b_v$ and sum. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cube-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-cube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cube): Ray coercivity, Neumann subdivision, and retained-coordinate slicing -> Cube and torus-cell kinetic penalties
- **step-close-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-close) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-close): Average translated grids and apply ray coercivity to coordinates sharing a cube -> Close-neighbor kinetic penalty
- **step-entropy** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy): Ideal Bose grand trace and the Gibbs variational inequality -> Entropy finiteness and kinetic control
- **step-insertion** [claim](#stmt-lem-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-insertion): Normalize a nearest-position cutoff configurationwise and cancel old kinetic cross terms -> Admissible insertion preserving the old marginal
- **step-increments** [claim](#stmt-prop-increments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-increments): Combine local energy and entropy, marginal entropy monotonicity, and an insertion frame -> Particle-number free-energy comparisons
- **step-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-localization): Isometric color localization, dephasing, and sector entropy subadditivity -> Localized free-energy and color-entropy inequalities
- **step-tilt** [claim](#stmt-lem-tilt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tilt): Compare equal-spectrum tilted states and discard a classical partition label in relative entropy -> Upper bound on diagonal-tilt free-energy excess
- **step-local-penalty** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-penalty) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-penalty): Average exponential localization masks and absorb linear count losses by a scalar square -> Weighted local free-energy penalty
- **step-field-density** [claim](#stmt-cor-field-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-field-density): Evaluate the penalty at equilibrium and discard its nonnegative close-count term -> Density control in bounded attractive fields
- **step-low-count** [claim](#stmt-prop-low-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-low-count): Fill localized empty regions, derive a shortage penalty, and integrate a negative-tilt moment derivative -> Joint exponential shortage bound
- **step-close-count** [claim](#stmt-prop-close-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-close-count): Use a Lipschitz close-count enlargement with bounded nearest-neighbor overlap and a positive tilt -> Joint close-count tail
- **step-purification** [claim](#stmt-prop-purification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-purification): Compose killed labelled kernels with the symmetrizer and split the slab at its midpoint -> Normalized thermal path law and exact occupation overlap
- **step-free-cutoffs** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-cutoffs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-cutoffs): Gaussian maximal estimates, centered increment moments, and bridge decomposition -> Free-motion and bridge regularity estimates
- **step-marked-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-kernel): Bin exits, combine Gaussian and rare-event kernel bounds, and retain a positive tube-staying probability -> Localized marked-path kernel comparison
- **step-sandwich** [claim](#stmt-lem-sandwich) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sandwich): Differentiate a Lipschitz form evolution and apply it to exterior powers before integrating positive-part inequalities -> Heat-trace comparison for every positive power
- **step-trajectory-moment** [claim](#stmt-prop-trajectory-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-trajectory-moment): Mark labels, remove incident exclusions, introduce colors, and pay marked occupations using positive gaps -> Interacting trajectory moment proportional to tested-site count
- **step-certificates** [claim](#stmt-prop-certificates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-trajectory-moment-2): Apply trajectory moments to visitor, diameter, and irregularity failures and combine them with endpoint tails -> Unconditional joint certificate supply
- **step-gaussian-packing** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-packing): Count separated Gaussian means using disjoint balls and annuli -> Averaged bridge-density bound
- **step-averaged-hit** [claim](#stmt-lem-averaged-hit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-averaged-hit): Exclude endpoint windows and sum necessary collision balls over mesh intervals using label and midpoint averaging -> Averaged obstacle-collision estimate
- **step-single-hit** [claim](#stmt-lem-single-hit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-single-hit): Repeat the collision-ball argument with a single endpoint density and reverse the whole bridge when needed -> Vanishing single-opponent collision bound
- **step-success-supply** [claim](#stmt-cor-success-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-success-supply): Union-bound nearby obstacles and use successive averaging bounds over labels and target points -> Many labels with large successful target sets
- **step-useful-labels** [claim](#stmt-lem-useful-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-useful-labels): Discard endpoint and diameter defects and apply the successful-label bound to the entire eligible list -> Eligible and strongly successful labels in certified cells
- **step-conditional-law** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-law): Disintegrate at endpoints, eligibility, allocations, and frozen paths; cancel fixed endpoint winding weights -> Exact conditioned product reference law
- **step-group-supply** [claim](#stmt-prop-group-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-group-supply): Sample eligible labels without replacement and combine conditional sampling failures with unconditional certificate failures -> Joint bad-site bound for each retained group
- **step-group-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-group-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-group-comparison): Disintegrate the midpoint Gaussian, lower-bound its success normalizer, and apply the single-opponent estimate after midpoint averaging -> Uniform density bound and deterministic compatibility
- **step-skeleton** [claim](#stmt-lem-skeleton) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-skeleton): Reveal aligned dyadic blocks, obtain summable conditional densities, and control ordered test moments -> Hard and soft skeleton encounter moments
- **step-lattice-boundary** [claim](#stmt-lattice-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lattice-boundary): Use coordinate rays for the fill and the cited boundary-connectivity theorem for the exterior shell -> Connected good detour shell with controlled size
- **step-small-lifts** [claim](#stmt-lattice-small-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lattice-small-lifts): Lift a spanning tree and rule out nonzero periodic edge discrepancies by the component-size bound -> Finite bijective periodic lifts
- **step-good-routes** [claim](#stmt-prop-good-paths) / [proof](#pm-h-41): Reject large or endpoint-enclosing components, detour around finite shells, erase loops, and average connected-component charges -> Simple good-route kernel with annealed encounter control
- **step-affinity** [claim](#stmt-lem-affinity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affinity): Apply Hölder to the common density and both squared likelihood integrals -> Positive affinity lower bound
- **step-tag-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-tag-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tag-normalization): Sum disjoint tag contributions, project the exposed data away, and use affinity monotonicity and superadditivity -> Cell-affinity lower bound from group affinities
- **step-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cancellation): Compare reference and proposal normalizers after deleting near factors and integrate normalized far densities -> Overlap-dependent likelihood product bound
- **step-scaled-occupation** [claim](#stmt-thm-scaled) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cancellation-2): Construct common midpoint assignments, average route likelihoods before squaring, average label matches, and sum affinities over groups and cell pairs -> Uniform positive scaled occupation fraction
- **step-physical-condensation** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:physical): Choose physical density and temperature, dilate to integer scaled boxes, and use the locally uniform volume threshold -> Positive-temperature condensation at fixed physical exclusion distance and density

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-fixed-physical-parameters**: The positive occupation bound concerns the exact canonical hard-sphere Gibbs state with exclusion distance, positive density, and positive temperature fixed before the thermodynamic limit.
- **invariant-scaled-uniform-occupation**: The scaled occupation fraction has a positive lower bound uniform over the stated temperature and exclusion-parameter ranges once the density and volume thresholds hold.
- **invariant-conditioned-product-law**: Exposing the recorded data leaves a product of individual reference path laws conditioned on mutual avoidance; the retained trajectories satisfy their individual regularity and confinement restrictions.
- **invariant-midpoint-averaging**: The collision estimate integrates the proposed midpoint over its target cell. Restriction to a set of controlled positive volume and conditioning on an event of controlled positive probability incur the stated change-of-measure costs.
- **invariant-common-environment-encounters**: The encounter moment averages over a common environment and independent conditional route draws, retaining the routing event's original mass.
- **invariant-normalizer-cancellation**: Uniform insertion compatibility and separation of far factors confine the likelihood product cost to shared indices and near factors. Reduced systems delete carried coordinates and their incident checks.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-critical-temperature-prediction**: Treat the existence temperature as a prediction of the phase transition or leading depletion. -> The occupation theorem supplies a positive condensate fraction at sufficiently low temperature without determining those physical quantities.
- **anti-changing-parameters-with-volume**: Replace the fixed-parameter thermodynamic conclusion by a calculation in which density, exclusion distance, or temperature changes with volume. -> Such a calculation does not establish the theorem's fixed physical regime.
- **anti-prescribed-midpoint-collision-bound**: Apply the averaged bridge collision bound uniformly at a prescribed midpoint. -> A midpoint coinciding with the obstacle at the joining time forces a collision.
- **anti-independent-retained-paths**: Sample retained reference trajectories independently and discard their mutual avoidance condition. -> The resulting law omits the interacting conditional normalizer and permits forbidden collisions.
- **anti-quenched-route-guarantee**: Interpret the encounter bound as holding uniformly for every fixed environment or after normalizing the routing event to full probability. -> The supplied bound averages the environment with the event indicator and does not give either strengthened guarantee.
- **anti-global-outside-normalization**: Normalize the coupled proposal only after integrating over the unselected outside paths. -> That normalization changes the outside marginal and invalidates the comparison construction.
- **anti-reintroducing-deleted-auxiliaries**: Replace a deleted auxiliary path instance by the original reference path carrying its label. -> The reduced proposal then has different obstacles and normalizers from those used in the cancellation argument.
- **anti-finite-sample-thermodynamic-certificate**: Certify the full condensation theorem by agreement on finitely many discretized configurations or simulated paths. -> Finite agreement does not decide the continuum Gibbs-state occupation bound or its thermodynamic lower limit.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The central result is a continuum canonical Gibbs-state occupation bound in the thermodynamic limit, rather than a finite constructive computation. Its source explicitly requires: "The exclusion distance, density, and temperature are held fixed in this limit." The proof combines continuous Brownian bridge measures, heat-semigroup traces, sufficiently large parameter thresholds, and an occupation lower limit. Finite enumeration or path discretization would check a restricted approximation without deciding this result. A finite executable certificate for the full theorem is ABSENT.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The small-component proof gives a finite construction for lifting a periodic graph component and checking consistency of its edges. This can support software that validates periodic lattice geometry under the stated size restrictions. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The trajectory laws describe imaginary-time quantum equilibrium and conditioned Brownian bridges. They do not supply real-time motion, collision responses, or gameplay dynamics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local certificates depend on complete continuous trajectories, their regularity, and hard avoidance. The paper does not provide a finite observation procedure that certifies those conditions from sampled positions. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The exterior-shell argument constructs a good nearest-neighbor route from a supplied finite walk by replacing obstructed segments and erasing loops. Its deterministic transformation is usable when the component lifts and endpoint exclusions have already been established. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The component sets, occurrence lists, and shells describe mathematical objects used by the route construction. The paper establishes no storage representation, update interface, or data-structure performance contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The heat-semigroup and exterior-power estimates bound analytic traces. They do not construct an accelerator implementation or give hardware resource requirements. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The common-measure inequality controls affinity through likelihood moments. Its conclusion supplies no adversary model, cryptographic primitive, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The diagonal tilt compares a modified canonical Gibbs state with its equilibrium free energy. It provides no learning objective, training procedure, or generalization contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The route construction guarantees admissible paths and controls encounters after probabilistic averaging. It neither minimizes route length nor solves a stated routing objective. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Macroscopic occupation of the constant orbital is a property of the exact symmetric quantum Gibbs state. The paper gives no communication, scheduling, or fault model from which a distributed consensus guarantee could follow. |
| other (mathematical physics) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The thermal slab explicitly constructs a positive path measure whose midpoint and retained-configuration density represents the square root of the canonical Gibbs state. This gives a rigorous framework for comparing constant-orbital occupation, although it does not provide a finite exact numerical sampler. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-dilute-physical-regime** (hypothesis): The condensation conclusion concerns sufficiently small fixed positive density and a fixed positive exclusion distance, with the physical temperature fixed before volume growth. Blocked use: ['Infer condensation at arbitrary density.', 'Replace the fixed-parameter conclusion by a coupled dilution and volume limit.']
- **limit-bosonic-dirichlet-model** (formal_scope): The Hamiltonian uses the symmetric hard-core Dirichlet form domain and zero extension. The theorem does not establish an occupation guarantee for a different particle-statistics space or a different contact-boundary convention. Blocked use: ['Transfer the result directly to antisymmetric or distinguishable-particle states.', 'Replace strict hard exclusion by a soft interaction without a separate argument.']
- **limit-dimensional-geometry** (formal_scope): The supplied continuum and lattice arguments use three-dimensional geometry. They do not establish the corresponding condensation or routing conclusions in another dimension. Blocked use: ['Claim positive-temperature condensation on a planar sample from this theorem.', 'Transfer the Gaussian packing and exterior-shell estimates to another dimension without proving replacements.']
- **limit-scaled-temperature-and-thresholds** (hypothesis): The scaled occupation bound requires the specified exclusion-parameter window, the temperature ceiling, and sufficiently large density and volume. Local uniformity of the volume threshold does not supply an arbitrary-volume guarantee. Blocked use: ['Apply the occupation bound outside the scaled temperature window.', 'Assert the same lower bound below the density or volume thresholds.']
- **limit-no-transition-or-depletion-prediction** (formal_scope): The occupation theorem does not determine the transition temperature or leading depletion. Blocked use: ['Interpret the furnished positive temperature as the critical temperature.', 'Use the occupation lower bound as a depletion asymptotic.']
- **limit-finite-energy-and-localization** (hypothesis): The entropy and localization comparisons require finite kinetic energy and the stated Lipschitz masks. Inside localization must preserve the prescribed spatial Dirichlet condition. Blocked use: ['Apply the entropy comparison to a state without verified finite kinetic energy.', 'Localize using discontinuous masks while omitting the gradient cost.', 'Discard the inside spatial boundary condition.']
- **limit-insertion-smallness-and-symmetry** (hypothesis): Normalized insertion requires control of the old positions near the orbital support and the excluded-volume smallness condition. It preserves the old position marginal but does not assert symmetry in the new coordinate. Blocked use: ['Insert a particle when the orbital-support crowding condition is unverified.', 'Treat the inserted labelled function as already fully bosonic.']
- **limit-local-field-strength** (hypothesis): The local penalty and field-density conclusions use the restricted field strength and particle-number range stated there. The broader field allowance in the insertion-increment bound does not extend these local conclusions. Blocked use: ['Apply the field-density estimate to an arbitrary-strength attractive field.', 'Extend the local penalty beyond its stated particle-number range solely from the insertion bound.']
- **limit-unconditional-spatial-and-certificate-laws** (hypothesis): The joint spatial and certificate estimates are unconditional bounds under the actual equilibrium and thermal sample laws. They are not uniform statements after arbitrary conditioning on outside positions or on certificate events. Blocked use: ['Condition on an arbitrary outside configuration and retain the same shortage probability.', 'Include full-sample certificates in exposed data while assuming the unchanged conditional bridge law.']
- **limit-thermal-slab-and-retained-configuration** (hypothesis): The thermal slab must fit within the inverse-temperature interval. The later mixed-state comparison retains the additional configuration throughout the observations. Blocked use: ['Use an outer heat kernel with a nonpositive time interval.', 'Resample the retained configuration between the compared observations.', 'Substitute a ground-state eigenfunction for the mixed-state purification.']
- **limit-color-smallness-and-marked-traces** (hypothesis): The interacting trajectory-moment estimate requires the color-smallness condition and the marked-bin coefficients of the preceding construction. Dropping incident hard-core constraints is an upper-bound step rather than an equality of interacting laws. Blocked use: ['Use the trajectory moment with arbitrary marking coefficients.', 'Sample the enlarged noninteracting marked colors as though they were the exact original path law.']
- **limit-regularity-separation-and-cutoff** (hypothesis): The collision estimates require regular obstacles, separated outer endpoints, and the replacement cutoff event. They do not bound unrestricted collisions with an arbitrary deterministic path. Blocked use: ['Use the vanishing collision bound against an irregular obstacle.', 'Drop endpoint separation.', 'Remove the confinement and regularity event from the probability being bounded.']
- **limit-prescribed-midpoint-counterexample** (counterexample_scope): The target-cell collision estimate averages the midpoint. A prescribed midpoint equal to the obstacle midpoint forces collision; restricting or conditioning the proposal requires the stated positive-volume or positive-probability cost. Blocked use: ['Claim a collision estimate uniform in every prescribed midpoint.', 'Ignore the change-of-measure cost of restricting the target set.']
- **limit-conditioned-product-not-independence** (hypothesis): The unexposed trajectories obey a product law conditioned on mutual avoidance, with positive normalizers for almost every sampled exposed datum. This does not establish independence or positivity for every conceivable datum. Blocked use: ['Discard mutual avoidance when sampling the retained group.', 'Assert positive conditional normalizers on arbitrary unsampled exposed configurations.']
- **limit-exposed-only-target-selection** (hypothesis): Proposal target sets are chosen from exposed data before sampling other unexposed trajectories. Shared-midpoint proposals have marginal density and collision bounds, without a product density bound or a bound conditional on the midpoint value. Blocked use: ['Choose target sets by inspecting latent opponent trajectories.', 'Multiply the marginal density bounds to assert a joint product-density bound for shared-midpoint paths.', 'Impose a collision check between path instances belonging to separately constrained copies.']
- **limit-annealed-routing-and-endpoints** (hypothesis): The route guarantee uses admissible endpoint pairs, the joint bad-set bound, and an acceptance event retaining its original mass. The encounter estimate averages one common environment and fresh independent route draws; it is not a bound for every fixed environment. Blocked use: ['Claim the same encounter bound for arbitrary endpoint pairs.', 'Normalize the acceptance event to full mass without accounting for the change.', 'Infer a worst-environment route guarantee.']
- **limit-finite-lifts-and-detour-preconditions** (hypothesis): Periodic component lifting requires the stated range and size restrictions. The exterior-shell detour additionally requires endpoints outside filled obstacle components and good connected replacement shells. Blocked use: ['Lift a wrapping component as though it projected bijectively.', 'Detour from an endpoint enclosed by a filled bad component.', 'Infer a shortest-path guarantee from the shell replacement.']
- **limit-preserving-outside-marginal** (hypothesis): The coupled proposal normalizes the remaining checks separately for every fixed collection of outside paths. A single normalization after integrating those paths changes their marginal. Blocked use: ['Replace conditional normalization by a global rejection sampler while assuming the original outside marginal.']
- **limit-cancellation-uniformity-and-deletion** (hypothesis): Conditional-normalizer cancellation requires uniform reference-coordinate and whole-factor insertion compatibility, separation of far factors, and deletion of carried coordinates with their incident checks. Matching particle labels does not identify auxiliary path instances with original reference coordinates. Blocked use: ['Apply cancellation using compatibility only on configurations already satisfying every pairwise check.', 'Delete only the primary coordinate of a factor.', 'Reintroduce original trajectories as obstacles after deleting near factors.']
- **limit-positive-common-measure-and-tag-sums** (hypothesis): The affinity argument needs a common absolutely continuous measure of positive mass with bounded squared likelihood integrals. Tag normalization uses disjoint groups and sums tag contributions rather than selecting a uniformly random tag. Blocked use: ['Infer positive overlap from a common measure of zero mass.', 'Use likelihood moments without absolute continuity.', 'Replace the tag sum by a normalized tag average and retain the same prefactor.']
- **limit-order-of-parameter-choices** (nonconstructive): The proof fixes local scales, path duration, proposal density bounds, and group size before the density threshold and then the volume threshold. Its positive bound inherits sufficiently-small and sufficiently-large choices rather than a fully specified numerical parameter certificate. Blocked use: ['Choose the group size after increasing density without rechecking compatibility.', 'Allow proof parameters entering the occupation bound to depend on the growing volume.', 'Present the formulas alone as numerical thresholds with all constants determined.']
- **limit-finite-computation-cannot-decide-full-theorem** (approximation): Finite lattice transformations can check restricted geometric statements, but finite path samples or discretized Gibbs calculations do not decide the continuum thermodynamic condensation conclusion. A finite exact numerical sampler and an approximation-error contract for the full theorem are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Treat finite simulation agreement as certification of the thermodynamic lower limit.', 'Transfer a discretized collision check to continuous strict avoidance without an error bound.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Bose–Einstein condensation at positive temperature in the three-dimensional dilute hard-sphere gas. For each fixed exclusion distance and every sufficiently small fixed density, there is a strictly positive temperature, independent of the volume, at which the exact canonical Gibbs state has a positive condensate fraction in the thermodynamic limit. The condensate occupies the constant orbital.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Bose–Einstein condensation is a statement about coherence across a macroscopic sample. For an interacting gas, local density estimates or accurate thermodynamic energies do not by themselves give that coherence. The question addressed here is whether a dilute three-dimensional gas with an impenetrable core has a macroscopically occupied orbital at a positive temperature that stays fixed as the volume grows.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The model and the result

Fix an exclusion distance $a>0$. On the flat torus $\Lambda_L=(\mathbb R/L\mathbb Z)^3$, let $d_L$ denote Euclidean torus distance and set $$\Omega_{N,L,a}
 =\{(x_1,\ldots,x_N)\in\Lambda_L^N:
                   d_L(x_i,x_j)>a\text{ whenever }i\ne j\}.$$ In units $\hbar^2/(2m)=k_B=1$, the hard-sphere Hamiltonian $H_{N,L,a}$ is the self-adjoint operator associated with $$q[f]=\sum_{i=1}^N\int_{\Omega_{N,L,a}}|\nabla_i f|^2,
 \qquad f\in H^1_0(\Omega_{N,L,a})\text{ symmetric}.$$ For $T>0$, its canonical state and one-particle density matrix are $$\Gamma_{N,L,T}=
 \frac{e^{-H_{N,L,a}/T}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-H_{N,L,a}/T}},\qquad
 \gamma^{(1)}_{N,L,T}=N\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{2,\ldots,N}\Gamma_{N,L,T}.$$ We use zero extension from the allowed configuration set when taking partial traces.
